ST_EEV_ACCSEEA	Implementation of standard accounting tools to monitor the economic and environmental aspects of tourism (SEEA tables)	250	France	0.0						Ministry of Economy		Energy: Pefa data are now available for 2014 to 2017. Air, waste, water: Reporting of AEA data has been mandatory since 2008, the data was transmitted to Eurostat from 2008 to 2017 (2018 soon). This is not requested, but we also have at our disposal the air emissions accounts drawn up by Citepa for the years 1990, 1995, 2000, 2005 but these years are not reported to Eurostat because the Eurostat questionnaire is not adapted (formatted in NACE rev.2 whereas these years are presented according to the NACE rev.1 nomenclature). RSD data is available for the years 2004, 2006, 2008, 2010, 2012, 2014, 2016 and 2018. The series of water statistics that we send to Eurostat start in 2000 for abstractions (except agriculture, 2008) and 2008 for polluting emissions. However, these accounts are not necessarily prepared in accordance with the SEEA from a formal point of view. ST_EEV_ACCSEEA	Implementation of standard accounting tools to monitor the economic and environmental aspects of tourism (SEEA tables)	300	Greece	0.0						Ministry of Tourism of Greece		"During 2017 a Project for the compilation of the Greek Tourism Satellite Account was conducted within the framework of the EU-funded âTechnical Assistance action to support tourism planning and policy for the promotion of sustainable tourism development in Greeceâ and in particular within the Activity I.1.1.: âDevelop monitoring and reporting tools for Greek Tourism: a national accounting and statistical systemâ. The main output of this project was the production of Pilot TSA Tables 1 to 4 for the year 2015, together with the documentation of gaps and deficiencies of the actual TSA-relevant database and proposals for improvements, indicating the necessary steps towards a routine-wise implementation of TSA in Greece in the future. The whole study is available in the following link: http://www.mintour.gov.gr/Statistics/meletes?pageNo=3 under the title ""Tourism Satellite Accounts: Potentials and Needs"". As a result of this study, in September 2019, the Ministry of Tourism of Greece signed a four-year Memorandum of Understanding with the Hellenic Statistical Authority (ELSTAT) in order to compile in common the first basic Tables (1 ĎÎż 4) of the Greek Tourism Satellite Account. The year of reference is 2015. The aim is to create time series starting from year 2015 and proceed with the coming years so as to produce TSA- tables in a periodic basis. The aforementioned MoU also referred to the setting up of two Joint Working Groups, consisting of Ministry and ELSTAT officials, willing to improve the quality of tourism statistics produced in Greece and to compile the TSA-Tables 1 to 4, as well. Environmental Accounts from ELSTAT are compiled according to EU regulation 691/2011. This regulation is in line with the accounting structures and principles of the SEEA central framework 2012, but there are some diffrences with the exact SEEA tables as reported in the SEEA cenral framework (in terms of classifications or definitions)."	C	Country data	NUMBER	Number	G	Global	2008				4	39	Southern Europe	Country	22.58307827	39.47301873	GRC	1	1

ST_EEV_ACCSEEA	Implementation of standard accounting tools to monitor the economic and environmental aspects of tourism (SEEA tables)	344	China, Hong Kong Special Administrative Region	1.0						Census and Statistics Department, HKSAR		"Inbound tourism ""consumption"" (instead of ""expenditure"") is included in the TSA Table. This is slightly different from the ""total tourism expenditure associated to inbound tourism"" released by the Hong Kong Tourism Board because the estimated visitors\' expenditures on departure tax and on services not directly provided by resident producers are not included in the ""inbound tourism consumption"".  Main aggregates on residents\' expenditure abroad (REA) are available in national account statistics. Main aggregates on gross output (at basic prices), intermediate consumption (at purchasers\' prices), value added (at basic prices), compensation of employees, other taxes less subsidies on production, gross operating surplus by selected tourism industries and other industries are available in national accounts statistics.  Tourism ratios for selected tourism products and selected tourism industries are available in TSA Table. Main aggregates on gross output (at basic prices), intermediate consumption (at purchasers\' prices), value added (at basic prices), compensation of employees, other taxes less subsidies on production, gross operating surplus by selected tourism industries and other industries are available in national accounts statistics. Employment numbers pertaining to the part provided to visitors by industries on retail trade, accommodation services, food and beverage services, transport services and other personal services are available. A similar ""Overall Energy Balance"", which is not exactly the same as ""SEEA physical flow accounts for energy"", is used." Main aggregates on gross output (at basic prices), intermediate consumption (at purchasers\' prices), value added (at basic prices), compensation of employees, other taxes less subsidies on production, gross operating surplus by selected tourism industries and other industries are available in national accounts statistics. Employment numbers pertaining to the part provided to visitors by industries on retail trade, accommodation services, food and beverage services, transport services and other personal services are available. A similar ""Overall Energy Balance"", which is not exactly the same as ""SEEA physical flow accounts for energy"", is used." ST_EEV_ACCSEEA	Implementation of standard accounting tools to monitor the economic and environmental aspects of tourism (SEEA tables)	372	Ireland	1.0						Central Statistics Office		Ireland does not currently compile a Toursim Satellite Account. CSO Environment Division complete annual Eurostat questionnaires on Inland waters, Energy (SEAI submit the Energy balances and we publish a Business Energy Use survey), Air emissions, and for the biennial Waste Statistics Regulation. These modules broadly follow the SEEA Central Framework. Separately, it may be possible for countries to use the Census to compile figures for total households and occupied households on Census night. Our Census is in April which is relatively off-season so the gap between total and occupied may give some idea of additional capacity for tourism and holiday homes which are probably more difficult to measure than hotels and guest-houses. Another data source that may be of interest are non-domestic Building Energy Ratings. There may be data available for European countries under the EPBD regulations  https://www.dccae.gov.ie/en-ie/energy/legislation/Pages/Energy-Performance-of-Buildings-Directive-(EPBD).aspx. Table 3 in this release shows the energy ratings for hotels etc. that have had an energy audit undertaken https://www.cso.ie/en/releasesandpublications/er/ndber/non-domesticbuildingenergyratingsq22020/. We also provide Irish Water with population data relating to wastewater agglomeration areas which they use to compare the capacity of a wastewater treatment plant with peak demand. The air emission figures are based on the air emissions inventories submitted annually by the Environmental Protection Agency (EPA) to the United Nations under the UNFCCC (United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change) and under the CLRTAP (Convention on Long-Range Transboundary Air Pollution). 	C	Country data	NUMBER	Number	G	Global	2008				4	154	Northern Europe	Country	-7.121425272	53.25274054	IRL	1	1

There may be data available for European countries under the EPBD regulations  https://www.dccae.gov.ie/en-ie/energy/legislation/Pages/Energy-Performance-of-Buildings-Directive-(EPBD).aspx. Table 3 in this release shows the energy ratings for hotels etc. that have had an energy audit undertaken https://www.cso.ie/en/releasesandpublications/er/ndber/non-domesticbuildingenergyratingsq22020/. We also provide Irish Water with population data relating to wastewater agglomeration areas which they use to compare the capacity of a wastewater treatment plant with peak demand. The air emission figures are based on the air emissions inventories submitted annually by the Environmental Protection Agency (EPA) to the United Nations under the UNFCCC (United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change) and under the CLRTAP (Convention on Long-Range Transboundary Air Pollution). ST_EEV_ACCSEEA	Implementation of standard accounting tools to monitor the economic and environmental aspects of tourism (SEEA tables)	620	Portugal	2.0						Instituto Nacional de EstatĂ­stica, National Accounts (all projects), National Inventory Report (Air emission accounts), Annual questionnaires of energy statistics - Electricity and heat, Natural gas, Oil , Renewables, Coal (Physical energy flow accounts)		"The referred TSA estimates (E) or provisional versions (P) don\'t have the complete breakdown foressen by the TSA tables; only an estimate of total demand and total tourism supply are estimated ad published. TSA tables are based on the TSA:RMF 2008 but they don\'t match completly. The Portuguese TSA compiles the tables of the ""European Implementation Manual on Tourism Satellite Accounts"". Nevertheless it adopted some of the 2008 recommendations, being the most important, the allocation of business tourism expenditure to tables according to the residence of the visitor (tables 1, 2 or 3), instead of table 4, ""other components"". Air emission accounts and Phisical energy flow accounts are compiled according to Regulation (EU) No 538/2014 of the European Parliament and of the council of 16 Aprol 2014. According to whereas (4) of this regulation, the new modules being introduced by this regulation are fully in line with SEEA. "	C	Country data	NUMBER	Number	G	Global	2008				4	39	Southern Europe	Country	-7.961599811	39.68509315	PRT	1	1

ST_EEV_ACCSEEA	Implementation of standard accounting tools to monitor the economic and environmental aspects of tourism (SEEA tables)	642	Romania	1.0						National Institute of Statistics		Physical energy flow accounts (PEFA) are one of six modules in Regulation 691/2011 and reporting has become mandatory in 2017. Romania provides data every year, begining with 2013. In the years 2010 to 2014 a task force developed the PEFA questionnaire. The main concepts and principles of PEFA are documented in a PEFA manual and are widely based on the system of environmental-economic accounting 2012 â central framework (SEEA-CF) which matured around the same time as PEFA. Also, for Air emissions accounts (AEA) Romania provides data every year for periode 2018 - (n-2), where n is reference year. AEA are in line with the accounting structures and principles of the System of Environmental-Economic Accounting â Central Framework (SEEA-CF; UN et al. 2012) which is the internationally agreed standard for concepts, definitions, classifications, accounting rules and tables for producing internationally comparable statistics on the environment and its relationship with the economy. The SEEA framework follows an accounting structure similar to the System of National Accounts (SNA) and uses concepts, definitions and classifications consistent with the SNA in order to facilitate the integration of environmental and economic statistics. AEA are in line with the accounting structures and principles of the System of Environmental-Economic Accounting â Central Framework (SEEA-CF; UN et al. 2012) which is the internationally agreed standard for concepts, definitions, classifications, accounting rules and tables for producing internationally comparable statistics on the environment and its relationship with the economy. The SEEA framework follows an accounting structure similar to the System of National Accounts (SNA) and uses concepts, definitions and classifications consistent with the SNA in order to facilitate the integration of environmental and economic statistics. ST_EEV_ACCSEEA	Implementation of standard accounting tools to monitor the economic and environmental aspects of tourism (SEEA tables)	70	Bosnia and Herzegovina	0.0						Agency for Statistics of Bosnia and Herzegovina		Bosnia and Herzegovina does not possess the requested data. Currently, the Agency for Statistics of BIH is working on the development of comparable and reliable indicators of the demand-side tourism statistics in BIH in accordance with EU Regulation 692/2011. Establishment of regular demand survey is one of the main preconditions for TSA production in BIH. Given the importance of measuring the tourism sector in BIH, Agency has nominate the experimental calculation of TSA as one of the activities that will be supported through new IPA 2019 Twinning project.\n\ufeffEurostat has been developing and promoting environmental-economic accounts for several years using the SEEA Central Framework 2012. Regulation (EU) No 691/2011 on European environmental economic accounts aims to establish a set of environmental accounts. \nThere are two waves of accounts. A first wave of accounts comprises environmental taxes, air emissions accounts and economy-wide material flow accounts. A second wave of accounts comprises environmental goods and services sector accounts, environmental protection expenditure accounts and physical energy flow accounts. Handbooks, manuals, questionnaires and compilation guidelines exist for all these accounts.\nThrough IPA 2017 Multi-beneficiary Statistical Cooperation Programme,  Agency for statistics of BiH implemented pilot project 5.5 Environmental accounts.\n1. Objectives of the project\nSet up and start transmitting estimates to Eurostat for the first set of accounts established in Regulation 691/2011, primarly  air emission accounts (AEA), environmental taxes (ETEA) and economy-wide material flow accounts (EW-MFA) for BA. \nSo far BA has no activities with regard to air emission accounts (AEA), but considers the development of this account both possible and realistic, since the greenhouse gas (GHG) inventory is well developed, available and a perfect basis to achieve results for SEEA Air Emission Accounts. UNDP BA has supported the implementation of a national inventory and produced four reports for BA following UNFCCC formats, with time series for 1990 â 2014. Objectives of the project\nSet up and start transmitting estimates to Eurostat for the first set of accounts established in Regulation 691/2011, primarly  air emission accounts (AEA), environmental taxes (ETEA) and economy-wide material flow accounts (EW-MFA) for BA. \nSo far BA has no activities with regard to air emission accounts (AEA), but considers the development of this account both possible and realistic, since the greenhouse gas (GHG) inventory is well developed, available and a perfect basis to achieve results for SEEA Air Emission Accounts. UNDP BA has supported the implementation of a national inventory and produced four reports for BA following UNFCCC formats, with time series for 1990 â 2014. Currently, the Agency for Statistics of BIH is working on the development of comparable and reliable indicators of the demand-side tourism statistics in BIH in accordance with EU Regulation 692/2011. Establishment of regular demand survey is one of the main preconditions for TSA production in BIH. Given the importance of measuring the tourism sector in BIH, Agency has nominate the experimental calculation of TSA as one of the activities that will be supported through new IPA 2019 Twinning project.\n\ufeffEurostat has been developing and promoting environmental-economic accounts for several years using the SEEA Central Framework 2012. Regulation (EU) No 691/2011 on European environmental economic accounts aims to establish a set of environmental accounts. \nThere are two waves of accounts. A first wave of accounts comprises environmental taxes, air emissions accounts and economy-wide material flow accounts. A second wave of accounts comprises environmental goods and services sector accounts, environmental protection expenditure accounts and physical energy flow accounts. Handbooks, manuals, questionnaires and compilation guidelines exist for all these accounts.\nThrough IPA 2017 Multi-beneficiary Statistical Cooperation Programme,  Agency for statistics of BiH implemented pilot project 5.5 Environmental accounts.\n1. ST_EEV_ACCSEEA	Implementation of standard accounting tools to monitor the economic and environmental aspects of tourism (SEEA tables)	764	Thailand	0.0						Ministry of Sports and Tourism, Thailand		Ministry of Sports and Tourism, Thailand, has also compiled Table 7, Table 8, Table 9 and Table 10. In 2019, Ministry of Sport and Tourism has developed a compilation of TSA link SEEA, following the instruction in LINKING THE TSA AND THE SEEA:\nA TECHNICAL NOTE, UNWTO. The objective of this pilot task was to statistical purpose, to assessing the possibility to estimate TSA that incorporate SEEA. The resuluts of this pilot task were the estimation of 4 SEEA accounts both physical terms and monetary terms, preliminary GREEN tourism GDP was also acheived. In this case, SEEA was ad hoc compiled by Ministry of Sports and Tourism for our own use purpose. In Thailand, it is not yet clear cut that which organisation should be officially responsible for SEEA compilation. ST_EEV_ACCSEEA	Implementation of standard accounting tools to monitor the economic and environmental aspects of tourism (SEEA tables)	858	Uruguay	0.0						Ministry of Tourism of Uruguay		In 2009, the first Tourism Satellite Account exercise was presented based on the COU 2005. Based on that, the projections for each year are made using price and physical volume indices. The Central Bank of Uruguay is about to publish the COU 2012 so we have updated the first three tables to 2012 and we are waiting for the new information for the tables of production and internal consumption. Regarding the environmental accounts Uruguay only has the Agricultural Sustainability Account, but a battery of sustainable tourism indicators has been designed that will be applicable from 2020.
